Because `WeekProgressionService` and `GateReadinessService` now need a session, the service also decides, until those batches, the dashboard's week states, Gate rows and video (a week in none of the participant's sessions is now shown locked with "You are not in a training session that includes this week." and no Gate or video links, and the "Program complete" banner checks the preferred session), the notifications' week access, Gate readiness, passed evaluation and video (a week in none of their sessions is now skipped, so a participant in no session gets no week notifications), and the coach participant page's Gate rows and Review Gate links (10.5). Delete it once 9.5, 9.9 and 10.5 carry the session. **Updated in 9.5:** the dashboard's week tabs moved to the session page, which carries its session, so neither the dashboard nor `ParticipantDashboardService` uses the service any more, and the week page's Back link goes to the session page. Its remaining callers are `ParticipantNotificationService` (until 9.9) and `CoachGateOverviewService` (the coach participant page's Gates, until 10.5); `sessionForWeek()` has no production caller left (only `ParticipantWeekSessionServiceTest` uses it). Delete the service, with its test, once 9.9 and 10.5 carry the session. **Updated in 9.9:** the notifications carry their session (one set per session), so `ParticipantNotificationService` no longer uses the service. Its only caller is `CoachGateOverviewService` (`sessionsOfProgram()` and `firstIncludingWeek()`, until 10.5); `sessionForWeek()` is still used only by `ParticipantWeekSessionServiceTest`. Delete the service, with its test, in 10.5. **Resolved in 10.5:** the coach participant page carries its session, so `CoachGateOverviewService` no longer uses the service; `ParticipantWeekSessionService` and `ParticipantWeekSessionServiceTest` are deleted.
- **The week page's Gate summary and its video still read across sessions** (found in 9.2) — **Resolved in 9.4:** both read the route's session only, as do the Gate and video links. ~~`TrainingWeekPageService::gateSummary()` (the user's evaluation) and its `WeeklyVideoRecording` lookup filter by user and Gate or week only; 9.4 names `WeekProgressionService` but not these two, so scope them to the route's session in 9.4, or session A's work shows on session B's week page. (`TrainingActivityPageService::attempts()`, the third lookup this entry named, was scoped to the session in 9.3.)~~
- **Attempt lookups that still read across sessions after 9.3** (found in 9.3). 9.3 scoped the participant submission pages, the activity page, the dashboard, the export and the retries. These still take a participant's latest attempt from any session: ~~`WeekProgressionService::requiresAttendanceForActivity()` and `requiresAttendanceForNewActivity()` (whether opening the activity needs a check-in; scope them when 9.4 gives every public method the session); `GateReadinessService` and `CoachGateReviewPageService` (Gate readiness from the latest attempts; 9.4 / 10.7);~~ (all three scoped to the session in 9.4, which leaves the mentor scope to 10.7) ~~`ParticipantNotificationService::sync()` (which draft, revision and review notifications are raised; 9.9);~~ (scoped to the session in 9.9) `ScoringService`, `CoachParticipantPageService` and the review queue (Phase 10; since 10.6 each queue row is one attempt shown with its session, and a coach sees only attempts in sessions where they currently mentor the participant); and ~~`TrainingWorkbookService::activityRows()` (the Drive workbook row of each activity), which no batch names yet~~ (scoped in 9.10: one workbook per session week, in that session's folder). 9.4 adds the missing submissions report (`MissingSubmissionReportService`: its week access now comes from the participant's sessions, but its activity and video checks still read every session; 10.9). ~~Decide where the workbook gets its session (one workbook per session, or a session column).~~ (9.10: a folder per session.) **Updated in 10.9:** the missing submissions report now checks activities, evidence and the weekly video in the row's session only.
- **Joining or leaving a session does not queue a notification sync** (found in 9.4) — **Resolved in 9.9:** `ParticipantProgressObserver` now observes `TrainingSessionParticipant` (created and deleted), so 9.7's join, 9.8's registration, the admin add and the admin remove each queue the participant's sync, and the sync resolves the active notifications of a session they left. Still picked up only on the participant's next progress write or the hourly run: an admin's change to a session's dates or number of weeks, and a week, activity or Gate an admin activates or deactivates. ~~Since 9.4, notifications cover only weeks in one of the participant's sessions, but `ParticipantProgressObserver` watches no `training_session_participants` writes, so a participant an admin adds (8.5) gets their week notifications only on their next progress write or the hourly `GenerateParticipantNotificationsJob` run, and a removed participant keeps theirs until 9.9 resolves them. Decide whether `TrainingSessionService` (add, remove and 9.7's join) queues the sync, in 9.7 or 9.9. **Updated in 9.7:** `join()` does not queue it either, so a participant who joins gets that session's week notifications only on their next progress write or the hourly job; left for 9.9.~~
- **Session-wide progress counts on coach pages before Phase 10** (found in 9.4). — **Resolved in 10.9:** the dashboard's COMPLETED counts the viewer's memberships that passed every active week of their session (any program), and the missing submissions report has one row set per membership, each checked against that session's weeks and work. ~~`WeekProgressionService` no longer reads a participant's progress across sessions, so two coach pages that 9.4 does not scope otherwise now read it per membership: the coach dashboard's "completed" count is the participants who passed every week of one of their sessions of the active program (before: every active week of the program, from any session's reviews; passes split across two sessions no longer count), and the missing submissions report lists a week when the participant can open it in one of their sessions of the active program that includes it (before: by the Gate chain over every session; a participant in no session of the program now has no rows). 10.9 adds the session filter and mentor scope; decide there whether the report gets one row set per session.~~
- **The Gate board does not name a row's session** (found in 9.4) — **Resolved in 10.7:** the board has a "Training session" column (title and dates) next to the session filter. ~~The board now has one row per session membership, so a participant in two sessions has two rows that look the same apart from their status and link. 10.7 adds a session filter and the gates CSV column; decide whether the board table also shows the session title.~~
- **Replacing a weekly video can unshare a file still chosen in another session** (found in 9.4). Choosing a different file removes the earlier file's domain share (8.16) for that row only. If the participant chose the same Drive file in sessions A and B and later replaces it in B, the share is removed although A's row still points at the file and says it is shared. Decide whether `WeeklyVideoRecordingService::choose()` keeps the share while another of the participant's rows uses the file.
- **The activity export picks an arbitrary attempt** (found in 9.3). `ActivityExportController` takes `first()` of the participant's attempts at the activity (now within the session) with no order, so after a retry it may export attempt 1 instead of the latest. Not changed in 9.3 (behavior outside the item); order by `attempt_number` descending if the latest attempt is what should be exported.
- **Stored notification links to the old week and activity URLs** (found in 9.2). `participant_notifications.action_url` stores absolute URLs; rows written before 9.2 point at `/training/weeks/{id}` and `/training/weeks/{id}/activities/{id}`, rows written before 9.3 at `/training/weeks/{id}/activities/{id}/submission` (draft, revision, review and approval notifications), and rows written before 9.4 at `/training/weeks/{id}/gates/{id}` (requirements reminder) and `/training/weeks/{id}/video-recording` (missing video reminder), which now 404. Active notifications are rewritten on the next sync (progress change or the hourly job); resolved notifications keep the dead link. Decide whether a deploy step re-syncs every participant or whether old links can stay (9.9 rewrites the keys and URLs per session anyway). **Resolved in 9.9:** every sync first deletes the participant's notifications whose key has no `session:` prefix, active and resolved alike (every row written before 9.9, including those with the dead links above), then writes the per-session ones, so after the first sync after deploy (the participant's next progress write, their joining or removal, or the hourly `notifications:sync-participants` run) no stored notification has an old key or URL. Resolved ones are therefore deleted, not kept: their history goes, and a reminder or event that still applies comes back under its new key as unread (even if the participant had read it). Production has few such rows (2.1 deleted every participant). The delete is one query per sync; remove `deleteNotificationsWithoutSession()` once production has synced every participant after the deploy. Notifications 9.9 resolves because the participant left a session keep their nested link (403 while they are out of the session, working again if they are added back).
- **The landing page keeps two inline scripts** (found in 9.5). `participant/dashboard` still carries the attendance card's live "Elapsed" timer script and the "What's Missing" disclosure's `aria-expanded` sync script inline. 9.5 moved them with the card unchanged; the code quality rules want shared behavior as named initializers in `resources/js/app.js`. Decide whether to move them (with `AttendanceTest`'s script assertions) in a cleanup batch.
- **The attendance card still says "training session"** (found in 9.5). 9.5 renamed the card's eyebrow to "ATTENDANCE" as the Assumptions ask, but its heading still reads "LAST TRAINING SESSION" after a check-out and its help text says "Check in when you begin your training session." (both asserted by `AttendanceTest`), which clashes with the new term. Decide whether they become e.g. "LAST CHECK-IN" and "Check in when you start training." (6.x wording or a small batch). **Still open after curriculum-sessions-merge**, which superseded the session term and left attendance (`AttendanceSession`) alone; the same decision is recorded in that plan's Open decisions.
- **Admins and coaches cannot open the participant session page** (found in 9.5). `training.sessions.show` is `role:participant` (Design: participant route), so an admin uses `admin.sessions.show` and a coach has no session page (Assumptions: "My sessions" card in 10.9). 11.6 says mentors and admins see every resource "at any time"; that holds on the admin tab, but if 11.6 means the participant page too, the route needs a non-participant view of it. Confirm when 11.6 starts. — **Answered for 11.6 without a new route:** the week rule is applied only on the participant page; the admin tab lists every link at any time (tested). Coaches still see no session resources anywhere (no coach session page, and the participant page 403s for them), so "mentors see every link" has nothing to show. Decide whether mentors need a read-only resources view (e.g. on the coach participant page or a "My sessions" row).
- **Available trainings include sessions of inactive programs** (found in 9.7). The landing page's Available trainings (and `TrainingSessionPolicy::join`) offer every not-completed session, whatever its program's `is_active`, as the item asks. A session whose program was deactivated (see "A session whose program was deactivated cannot be edited", 8.3) can therefore still be joined. Decide whether joining (and 9.8's registration list) should skip sessions of inactive programs. **Updated in 9.8:** registration offers and accepts the same sessions, through the new `TrainingSession::openToJoin()` scope that 9.7's Available trainings now uses too; a decision to skip inactive programs changes that scope and `TrainingSessionPolicy::join`.
- **Notifications do not name their session** (found in 9.9). A participant in two sessions of the same program gets one set of notifications per session with the same titles and bodies (e.g. two "Activity incomplete — {activity} has not been completed for Week 1."), told apart only by their links, and the dashboard's "What's Missing" groups them by the "Week N" in the title, so both sessions' Week 1 items share a group. Decide whether the body names the session (e.g. "… in {session title}") or the dashboard groups by session.
- **Drive tracking rows and folders from before 9.10** (found in 9.10). Pre-9.10 `google_drive_exports` rows have scope keys `1` to `4` (`week_folder`, `weekly_workbook_nested`, `week_evidences_folder`) and `{week}:{activity}` (`evidence_activity_folder`). The new keys never match them, so the first sync after 9.10 builds the session tree beside the old top-level `Week N` folders, which stay in the participant's Drive and are no longer updated; their rows stay in the table unused (nothing reads them; `ExportLifecycleService::reconcileStale()` only touches active statuses). Production has none: 2.1 runs in the same release, empties the table and deletes every participant. Only local and staging databases where a participant synced between 2.1 and 9.10 have them. Decide whether to delete those rows (a small migration on `export_type` plus a scope key without `session:`) or leave them; the Drive folders are the participant's to delete.
- **A session's Drive folder keeps its first name, and stops updating when the participant leaves** (found in 9.10). The session folder is tracked by ID and `ensureFolder()` reuses a tracked folder whatever its name, so renaming a session (8.3) does not rename the folder in participants' Drives, and a participant who later joins a second session with the same title gets the disambiguated name only for the new folder. A participant removed from a session is no longer synced for it (memberships only), so that folder keeps its last contents; adding them again resumes it. Every run also re-uploads every workbook of every session the participant is in, completed ones included, so the Drive requests per sync grow with their sessions. Decide whether renames should rename the folder, and whether completed sessions should stop syncing.
- **Removing a participant loses their repository link** (found in 9.11). `repository_url` lives on the membership row, which removal deletes (Design), so after an admin removes a participant the mentor's review, scoring and checklist pages for their work in that session say "No repository yet", and adding them again does not bring the link back (they must add it again on the session page). Decide whether that is acceptable or the link should outlive the membership.
- **A saved repository can be changed but not cleared** (found in 9.11). The field is required on save, so a participant who added a wrong repository can replace it but not remove it. Decide whether a "Remove" action is needed.
- **The repository shows only on the three mentor pages** (found in 9.11). 9.11 adds it to submission review, scoring and the Gate checklist as asked; the admin session page's Participants tab, the coach participant page and the Gate board do not show it. Decide whether 10.x (mentor scope and session filters) should list it there too.
- **No way to unassign a participant's mentor** (found in 10.3). The Mentors tab can assign and change a mentor but not set a participant back to "Not assigned": `AssignMentorRequest` requires a session mentor, and the only ways to end a current assignment are removing the mentor or the participant from the session. MEN-2 only requires that a participant can be without a mentor until one is assigned, so this was left out. Decide whether admins need an "Unassign" action (it would call `MentorAssignmentService::endCurrentAssignment()` and need its own audit action, e.g. `session_mentor_unassigned`).
- **A mentor removed during an assignment fails in the default error bag** (found in 10.3). If a mentor is removed from the session between `AssignMentorRequest`'s check and `MentorAssignmentService::assign()`, the service's `ValidationException` (`mentor_id`) redirects back with the default error bag, so the Mentors tab does not show it beside the participant's select (the request's bag is per participant). Rare; decide whether the controller should catch it and put it in `AssignMentorRequest::errorBagFor()`.
- **Deactivated or demoted coaches stay session mentors** (found in 10.2). Adding a mentor requires an active coach, but deactivating a coach or changing their role on the admin user form does not touch `training_session_mentors` or their current `mentor_assignments`, so they stay listed on the Mentors tab with their participants assigned. (10.4: `mentorsParticipantInSession()` requires the Mentor role, so a coach changed to another role loses mentor access although the assignment stays; a deactivated coach cannot sign in.) Decide whether deactivation or a role change should remove them as mentors (ending their assignments, as Remove does) or be refused while they mentor a session.
- **The admin user page no longer shows a participant's mentors** (found in 10.4). With the global assignment section removed, the only place an admin sees who mentors a participant is each session's Participants and Mentors tabs. Decide whether the admin user edit page should list the participant's sessions with their current mentor in each (read only, linking to the session's Mentors tab).
- **Participants in no session are no longer on the coach participants list** (found in 10.5). The list now has one row per session membership, so a participant who has not joined (or was removed from) every session is not listed, not even for admins, who still see them on the admin user list. Before 10.5 every participant was listed. Decide whether admins need them listed (without an Inspect link, since the participant page needs a session). **Updated in 10.8:** the same holds for the scoring overview and `participant-score-summary.csv`, which now list one row per membership. **Updated in 10.9:** the same holds for the coach dashboard's counts, the progress report and its progress and at-risk CSVs, and the missing submissions report, which count or list memberships (a participant in two sessions counts twice, e.g. in PARTICIPANTS and Total Participants).
- **A removed participant's work in a session cannot be opened from the coach pages** (found in 10.5). `coach.participants.show` binds the participant through the session's participants, so after an admin removes them the page is a 404, although their submissions, evaluations and videos keep the session (re-adding restores access). Decide whether admins should still open it (e.g. bind through the participant's work instead of the membership). **Updated in 10.8:** `coach.scoring.participant` binds the same way, so after removal that page is a 404 too, including the redirect after saving a score on the removed participant's work (`ScoringController::store()` redirects to the submission's session page); the scoring page itself (`coach.scoring.edit`) still opens for an admin.
- **The review queue's week and activity filters still come from the active program** (found in 10.6). `CoachReviewQueuePageService` offers the weeks and activities of `CoachReportService::activeProgramWeeks()` (the first active program), and the `week` filter matches a week number in any program, while the queue now lists attempts per session and can be filtered by session. Attempts in a session of another program can be listed but their activities are not offered in the activity filter. Decide whether the week and activity options should come from the selected session's program (or the programs of the viewer's sessions) when GEN-4's active-program lookups are replaced. The Gate board (10.7) has the same gap: its week filter offers the active program's weeks (`TrainingWeek::inActiveProgram()`) and matches a week number in any program, while its rows and session filter cover every session the viewer may see. The scoring overview and scoring reports (10.8) have the same gap: their week filter offers `TrainingWeek::inActiveProgram()` and matches a week number in any program, while their rows cover every session the viewer may see; the overview's week columns are week numbers too, so a "W2" column holds week 2 of each row's own program. The missing submissions report (10.9) has the same gap: its week filter offers `TrainingWeek::inActiveProgram()` and matches a week number in any program, while its rows cover every session the viewer may see. **Updated in 12.2:** the session dashboard's Activities tab links to the queue with `session` and `activity`; the filter applies to any activity ID, but for a session of a program that is not the active one the activity select shows "All" while the list is filtered by that activity.
- **The second-reader rule's running average reads every session** (found in 10.8). `ScoringService::runningAverage()` (the scoring page's "Previous running average" and the "more than 15 points from the running average" second-reader trigger in `ScoringController::store()`) averages the participant's published scores in every session, while the scoring pages and exports now summarize each session separately. Decide whether the running average should be the participant's average in the submission's session.
- **A completed week locked by an earlier incomplete review still shows as available** (found in 11.1). The session page (`ParticipantDashboardService::weeks()`: `available = passed || open`) and the notifications (`openWeeksBySession()`) keep treating a passed week as available, as before 11.1. If a mentor marks Week 1 incomplete after completing Week 2, Week 2 is `Locked` (`ensureCanAccessWeek()` 403s) but its tab still says "Completed" and its activity links show; the same holds for a week completed before its date. Decide in 11.2 whether such a week shows its lock (and its links go) or stays open for viewing. — **Resolved for the session page in 11.2:** WK-2 ("locks every later week") and 11.2 ("a review-locked week shows … instead of its contents", its URLs 403) make it clear, so a week is available on the page only while it is open (`available = isOpen()`): a completed week that is locked, or completed before its date, shows the lock icon and its message instead of "Completed" and its contents. **Still open for the notifications:** `ParticipantNotificationService::openWeeksBySession()` still keeps a passed week (`passed || open`), so a locked completed week keeps its activity, video and Gate notifications, and its "Week N completed" event links to the week page, which 403s. Decide whether notifications should follow `isOpen()` too (then they resolve while the week is locked).
- **The participant Gate page of a closed week still opens** (found in 11.2). `GateReviewController@participant` (`training.gates.show`) checks the session membership and that the Gate belongs to the week, but not `ensureCanAccessWeek()`, so a participant can open the Gate page (checklist status, readiness and deliverable links) of a week that has not opened or is locked by an incomplete review by URL or from a notification; the session page no longer links to it. 11.2 covered only the week, activity and submission URLs. Decide whether the Gate page should 403 with the week's locked message like the others.
- **A retry opened after the end cannot be worked on** (found in 11.4). The Assumptions limit reopening in a completed session to *Needs revision*, so `ActivitySubmission::isReopened()` does not count a retry attempt (a new `Draft` attempt without `submitted_at`). A mentor's retry of a failed attempt (`coach.reviews.retry`) or the approval of a recovery request made before the end (`coach.recovery.decide`) still opens one after the end (mentor abilities unchanged), but the participant's writes on it 403 with "This training session has ended." (superseded wording: "This curriculum has ended." since curriculum-sessions-merge 2.1), and new recovery requests are refused for that reason. Decide whether a retry a mentor or admin opens after the end counts as reopened (then `isReopened()` also accepts a draft attempt with an attempt number above 1, and recovery requests can stay open), or whether retries and recovery decisions should be refused in a completed session.
- **A draft that was never handed in cannot be read after the end** (found in 11.4). The submission form (`training.submission.edit`) 403s in a completed session unless the attempt is reopened, and `training.submission.show` 404s for drafts, so a participant's unsubmitted first draft is no longer readable once the session ends (handed-in versions, scores and the attempt list stay viewable). Decide whether the form should open read-only instead.
- **Two participant pages still assume an open session** (found in 11.4). With Google Drive readiness enforced, the activity page of an activity without an attempt still redirects a participant whose Drive is not ready to the dashboard, also in a completed session where nothing can be started; and the participant's weekly video help line ("Video not opening? … Choose it again.", `x-weekly-video-actions`) still says to choose it again after the end. Decide whether both should follow `TrainingSession::acceptsWork()`.
- **The "Waiting for verification" list also shows a last week and completed sessions** (found in 11.5). The card lists a membership whenever its locking review (11.1's rule) has new work since it, so it includes the incomplete review of a session's last week, although that review locks no later week and the participant's page shows no "Waiting for your mentor" (11.3 sets it only on locked weeks), and memberships of completed sessions, where only a reopened attempt can bring new work (11.4). Both still need the mentor to mark the week completed. Decide whether the card should list only weeks that lock a later week, or only sessions that have not ended; either is one condition in `CoachReportService::awaitingVerification()`.
- **Session overview counts and the review queue disagree after a participant is removed** (found in 12.1). The Overview tab counts only the session's current participants, but the review queue filtered by session (`CoachReportService::reviewQueue()`, which 12.2 plans to link to), the Gate board and the admin dashboard's global AWAITING REVIEW / NEEDS REVISION / PENDING GATES still count a removed participant's work, which keeps its session. Decide whether those lists should also leave out removed participants. **Updated in 12.2:** the Activities tab counts only current participants too, and each row links to `coach.reviews?session={id}&activity={activityId}`, whose list still includes a removed participant's attempts awaiting review or needing revision, so the queue can show more than the row's Awaiting review and Needs revision counts.
- **"Pending gates" counts only saved reviews that are not completed** (found in 12.1). As on the admin dashboard, it counts `gate_evaluations` rows that are not `Passed`; since 7.3 only *Completed* and *Incomplete* are saved, so it is in effect the incomplete reviews, and a week checklist waiting for its first review (the Gate board's derived "Ready for review", which needs readiness per membership) is not counted. Decide whether the overview should count checklists awaiting a first review instead or as well.
- **"Participants with locked weeks" still counts locks in a completed session** (found in 12.1). The count reads the `Locked` access state, which 11.4 keeps after the session ends although every week is then viewable. Decide whether a completed session's overview should show 0 there (or relabel it).
- **`StoreWeeklyVideosAsGoogleDriveFilesMigrationTest` would pass without the `drive_file_id` check** (found in 13.1). Its remaining case inserts a weekly video without `drive_file_id`, but also without `training_session_id`, which has been required since 9.4, so the insert would be refused even if `drive_file_id` were nullable. Decide whether to give the insert a session. The four `…MigrationTest` files that 13.1 kept (`DropTrainingPathColumnsMigrationTest`, `RequireTrainingSessionOnTrainingRecordsMigrationTest`, `StoreWeeklyVideosAsGoogleDriveFilesMigrationTest`, `TurnTrainingSessionResourcesIntoLinksMigrationTest`) now only check the squashed schema; decide whether to rename them or move their cases next to the models' other tests.
- **The "Developer program release" steps describe migrations no database will run** (found in 13.1) — **Resolved on 27 Oct 2026:** `docs/ATOMIC_DEPLOYMENT.md` was deleted. Its Google Drive setup and the two seeder commands moved to `docs/AWS_DEPLOYMENT.md` §8 "First release"; the QA-data deletion walkthrough and the migration-squash adoption steps were dropped because production (EC2 + RDS) starts from an empty database. ~~`docs/ATOMIC_DEPLOYMENT.md` still walks through deleting QA training data on a production database, but no production database exists and a post-squash release creates the final schema directly. 13.1 only added a note to its step 5. Decide whether to drop or shorten that section.~~
